  • Sources: https://sdlawlibrary.libguides.com/c.php?g=1290767&p=9818623 ; https://legalclarity.org/new-california-law-automatically-seals-criminal-records/ ; https://ccresourcecenter.org/state-restoration-profiles/california-restoration-of-rights-pardon-expungement-sealing/ (updated 13 Oct 2026) ; https://assets.nationbuilder.com/thesocialimpactcenter/pages/219/attachments/original/1752119664/JCOD_White_paper.pdf?1752119664 — accessed 1 Nov 2026 (all secondary).
  • Verify on leginfo: AB 1076 / SB 731 eligibility exclusions, waiting periods, and whether cannabis convictions already relieved under §11361.8 are excluded from Clean Slate counts. Do not publish an eligibility list from the white paper alone.
  • Still missing: Penal Code §1203.4 dismissal procedure, fees, and effect on employment/housing disclosures; whether a dismissed/sealed cannabis conviction must still be disclosed for state licensing, federal immigration, or firearms purposes — Verify, no legal advice.
  • Prop 36 (2024) note: a white-paper advocacy framing states Proposition 36 and other recriminalising laws “threaten to block progress” on record relief. That is not a statement that Prop 36 repealed AB 1793/SB 731. Verify Prop 36's effect on record relief separately; do not assert a repeal.

12.11 Social equity — consumer-facing programmes

  • REAL CA equity retailer finder: DCC equity page states consumers can find licensed equity retailers via the REAL CA Campaign search tool by ZIP/city, labelled “[Equity Retailer].” Exact REAL CA URL was Verify in Run 1 and remains Verify — do not invent it.
  • Source: https://www.cannabis.ca.gov/resources/equity/ — accessed 1 Nov 2026 (Run 1).
  • Community reinvestment: GO-Biz Community Reinvestment Grants, $50 million Tier 2 allocation in the Cannabis Tax Fund distribution (RTC §34019, Run 1 Part 2.5, verbatim text Verify). That $50m is a fund allocation, not a per-person consumer benefit — do not present it as an individual grant amount.
  • Business-facing equity relief (cross-reference Run 1 Parts 3–4, do not re-derive): DCC equity fee waivers/deferrals, 12-month eligibility, under-$5m renewal test, GO-Biz local grants. Part 12 must distinguish business-facing relief from consumer-facing equity-retailer identification.

12.12 Medical patient rights

  • SB 1186 (Stats. 2022, Ch. 395, effective 1 Jan 2023; operative prohibition from 1 Jan 2024), BPC §§26320–26325: §26322(a) — a local jurisdiction shall not adopt/enforce a regulation prohibiting retail sale by delivery of medicinal cannabis to patients/caregivers, or having the effect of prohibiting it in a timely and readily accessible manner and in types/quantities sufficient to meet demand. §26324 preserves full local control for adult-use delivery. §26325 — statewide concern, not a municipal affair. §26323 — enforcement by mandamus-type action (Code Civ. Proc. §§1084 et seq.) by a patient, caregiver, medicinal business, or other beneficially interested party. League of California Cities FAQ summary: prohibited local rules include limiting the number of businesses authorised for medicinal delivery, limiting hours, limiting number/frequency of delivery sales, requiring physical premises in the city; reasonable time/place/manner regulations consistent with state law/DCC rules are not barred.
  • Verify §§26320–26325 on leginfo before quoting; Run 1 relied on secondary reproductions for verbatim text.
  • MMIC vs recommendation (Run 1 anchors): recommendation (Prop 215, HSC §11362.5(d)) — written or oral physician recommendation/approval; no card required to claim Prop 215 protection (§11362.71(f)). MMIC (SB 420, HSC §§11362.71 et seq.) — CDPH programme, county-issued, 1 year, photo, 9-digit ID, county, expiration, state seal; required with government ID for the sales-tax exemption only. Caregiver: “primary caregiver” = individual designated by the patient who has consistently assumed responsibility for housing, health, or safety (§11362.5(e)); SB 420 patient-count rules were summarised, not opened — Verify §11362.7(d). Do not conflate MMIC issuance counts (Parts 5–6) with active patient rights.
  • Patient counts (Run 1, for context only): MMIC FY2025–26 3,056 issued, cumulative 128,210 (CDPH opened; CannBus 1,047 rejected).

12.13 Hemp consumer rules

  • Current rules (Run 1): since 23 Sep 2024 — no detectable THC per serving, 21+ only, ≤5 servings per package (CDPH DPH-24-005, Title 17 §§23000 et seq.). From 1 Jan 2026 (AB 8 Phase 1): extract in foods/beverages/supplements/pet food must be >99% CBD/CBN by COA, no THC, no synthetics; cosmetics extract no THC except topical ≤0.3%; hemp flower, pre-rolls, inhalable hemp-THC cannot be sold/offered/provided in California; manufacture solely for out-of-state sale not prohibited (IHEO + <0.3% COA). From 1 Jan 2028 (AB 8 Phase 2): extracting cannabinoids requires a DCC licence; CBD/CBN isolate-only food/supplement/pet-food manufacturers still CDPH IHEO; inhalable manufacturers move CDPH → DCC; intoxicating hemp treated as cannabis under MAUCRSA.
  • Labelling (AB 45): labels/QR/barcode linking to COAs with THC content; warning that children and pregnant/breastfeeding persons should consult health professionals; statement “THE FDA HAS NOT EVALUATED THIS PRODUCT FOR SAFETY OR EFFICACY”; ban on untrue health-related statements. AB 45 prohibited hemp in medical devices, prescription drugs, “processed smokable products,” and alcoholic beverages — section number Verify after AB 8 amendments.
  • What hemp products may NOT claim — state carefully: a product with detectable THC per serving may not be sold as a general-retail hemp food/beverage/supplement in California on 1 Nov 2026, even if ≤0.3% dry weight federally. A hemp-derived delta-8/10, THC-O, HHC, or synthetic cannabinoid product may not be sold outside the DCC system (Run 1). THCA flower may not be sold as hemp flower in California from 1 Jan 2026, because THCA is included in California's “THC” definition. A claim to diagnose, treat, cure, or prevent disease (unless FDA-approved drug) is a general FDA/FTC principle, not a California hemp-specific quote retrieved in this session — Verify against CDPH/FDA guidance before stating it as a California hemp labelling rule.
  • Still to research: CDPH hemp consumer complaint route (distinct from DCC cannabis complaints), CDPH hemp product recall route, and whether CDPH publishes a consumer-facing hemp product list. Do not assume DCC's recalls portal covers CDPH hemp products.

RESEARCH NOTES — BOTH RUNS (COMBINED)

Cut-off and date accessed: 1 November 2026 throughout, unless a source itself is dated differently and that date is stated. No 3 November 2026 election result is included anywhere in this file. This notes section covers Run 1 (Parts 0–6) and Run 2 (Parts 7–12). It is part of the dossier, not an appendix to be detached: every Verify, Unavailable, conflict and gap below applies to the Part that cites it.

N.1 Scope and method — what each run actually did

  • Run 1 (Parts 0–6) opened official pages where the research tool returned them (DCC, CDTFA, CDPH, CDFA, leginfo, State Auditor, FTB). Where a page could not be opened, or a fact rests only on a secondary summary, a legislative analysis, or a search extract, it is marked Verify or SECONDARY and is not presented as settled official text. Three browser.open attempts failed transiently and were NOT repeated (CDFA Registration Summary PDF, CDFA IHAB PDF, DCC compliance page) — facts from those are search-text only.
  • Run 2, Parts 7–8: official pages opened directly: CDFA FAQ, CDPH AB 8 FAQ, CDPH 8610 IH (3/2026), CDPH 8763 (3/2026), CDPH forms index, Fresno County hemp page, City of Desert Hot Springs Regulatory Permit Status List (1 May 2023), CEQAnet SCH 2023100184, Humboldt Legistar File #23-1473 (via search extract), DCC licence-search explainer. Everything else in Parts 7–8 is a search extract, a county/city document hosted secondarily, or broker marketing — labelled as such.
  • Run 2, Parts 9–10: most material rests on search extracts and secondary sources, NOT live page opens — a re-open attempt for the USDA AMS plan list, DCC About page, CCAP About page and CAC page returned an upstream browser 429 rate-limit and was not repeated; that failure does not prove those pages unavailable in a live browser. Items are labelled Opened in Run 1 / Search extract / Secondary / Verify accordingly — do not upgrade labels.
  • Run 2, Parts 11–12: no live browser visit was made in that research session. Material came from public-web text searches and two Cornell page text fetches (§15417 and §15409). Several regulation texts are Cornell LII / Rogoway reproductions, not the official Westlaw CCR text — DCC's own “Text of Regulations” page says its document covers regulations in effect as of 1 October 2026 and that Westlaw hosts the official version. Re-check every section and figure against that text before publication-grade use.
  • Run 1 file preserved separately unchanged at california-cannabis-hemp-run1-parts-0-6.html (path as delivered in Run 1: ~/workspace/your_files/california-cannabis-hemp-run1/california-cannabis-hemp-run1-parts-0-6.html). This complete file carries its Parts 0–6 content forward; where Run 2 corrects Run 1, the correction is printed explicitly (see §N.2), not silently overwritten.
  • Self-contained claim: this file contains no external CSS, JS, fonts or images. Source links are ordinary hyperlinks for the reader to open; no content is fetched from them when the file is opened offline.

N.2 Corrections to Run 1 made in Run 2 — printed explicitly, not silently overwritten

  1. AG §26308 opinion located (MAJOR CORRECTION). Run 1 said no AG notification under BPC §26308 was located. Parts 9–10 located AG Rob Bonta's 19 December 2023 opinion (36-page letter to DCC; DCC request 27 January 2023; opinion number/full text not verified — Verify on oag.ca.gov): conclusion YES, significant legal risk under the CSA. Consequence: §26308(a)(4) trigger is NOT satisfied; no interstate agreement in effect remains true, but the reason is now documented. Sources are secondary reports only.
  2. AB 1302: secondary evidence now says vetoed — still Verify veto date/message on leginfo. Run 1 had said AB 1302 (2021) restoring 15-mile did not become law, without a veto finding.
  3. Statewide buffer (§26054(b)): Run 1 framing said no single statewide buffer in DCC text opened, except advertising restrictions. Run 2 (Part 8, §8.1.2) corrects this: BPC §26054(b) sets a 600-foot statewide default radius from schools/day cares/youth centers, unless DCC or a local jurisdiction specifies a different radius. Verbatim leginfo text still Verify (FindLaw reproduction used).
  4. Farmer judgment still not opened. Opinion filed 20 November 2020, judgment 11 January 2021 — judgment itself still NOT opened; Verify on courts.ca.gov. This is an open item, not a correction of a Run 1 fact.
  5. Tribal store counts 14 vs 15 — range, secondary, no official count (see §N.3).
  6. USDA tribal hemp list: 2021 500nations list (with Ysleta Del Sur Pueblo — Texas — error) vs live AMS list (La Jolla confirmed in one extract; Cahuilla, Pala, Santa Rosa confirmed in the Part 7 AMS extract; full table pending).
  7. USPS hemp record retention 2 vs 3 years — conflict preserved.
  8. Kelly decision date 10 January vs 21 January 2010 — conflict preserved.
  9. Round Valley judge Illston vs Illman — conflict preserved.
  10. Proposition 207 California claim — wrong, excluded. Proposition 207 is Arizona's; California on-site consumption authority is AB 1775 (2024) + local opt-in.
  11. Riverside “Prop. 15” misprint — do not copy; it is Prop 215.
  12. UCETF series stay dated/separate — page snapshot, Q3 2025, January 2026 secondary, and October 2026 Governor series must never be merged into one total.
  13. CCAP agency name conflict (1 July 2026 reorganisation) — Verify.
  14. DCC leadership titles may have shifted (O'Connor CIO vs Acting Chief Deputy) — Verify live.
  15. Daily limits corrected: Run 1 note citing “§§15410 et seq.” for limits was wrong — limits are 4 CCR §15409 (Cornell page opened in Parts 11–12); returns are §15410. Cova formulation (“8 oz flower AND 8 oz concentrates”) rejected; §15409(b) is 8 ounces of medicinal cannabis in dried mature flowers or the HSC §11362.77 plant conversion + 12 immature plants, with recommendation override and no combining of adult-use and medicinal limits.

N.3 Conflicts preserved — do not resolve by picking one side silently

Run 1, Parts 5–6 conflicts (§6.10 of Run 1, carried forward)

  1. Active DCC licence totals: 7,744 (CannBus, Jan 2026, tracker) vs 8,252 (GrowerIQ, undated) vs ~7,630 (CaNORML, 2026) vs 8,514 active / 10,828 inactive (law-firm registry analysis, undated). Resolution: replace all with a dated DCC Licence Summary dashboard extract in the live browser.

N.4 Resolved in Run 2 — do not re-open as conflicts

  • Daily limits §15409 (adult 28.5 g / 8 g / 6 plants; medical 8 oz dried flowers or HSC §11362.77 conversion + 12 plants, recommendation override, no combining; returns are §15410).
  • Retail ID §15404 (government document with name/DOB/height/gender/photo, Armed Forces ID, or US/foreign passport; adult 21+, medical 18+ with recommendation; no ID-retention/scanning rule found in §15404 as returned; expired-ID treatment not addressed).
  • Open container VC §23222(b) (infraction ≤$100; trunk exception; patient/cardholder exception if carrying card/recommendation and container sealed/resealed/closed; plus VC §23221 no use while driving/riding) — leginfo text via search, full page open still required before verbatim quotation.
  • Public-use fines §§11362.3–11362.4 summaries ($100 public place; $250 tobacco-ban areas and 1,000-ft school/day care/youth-centre zone, children present; under-18 education + community service instead of fines — verbatim leginfo open still required).
  • Testing action levels §§15717, 15720, 15721, 15723, 15718, 15722, 15724 (pesticide numeric tables and §15724(h) tolerance still Verify).
  • Waste §17223 (methods + plan + secured storage; rendering “unusable” under video applies to failed batches, not pure plant waste; no 50/50 rule in the text opened).
  • Workplace exemptions in §12954 text (building/construction excluded; federal clearance excluded; no preemption of federal/state testing mandates covering DOT; no general safety-sensitive or law-enforcement exemption by name).
  • Statewide buffer §26054(b) 600-foot default (see §N.2.3).
  • AG §26308 opinion located (see §N.2.1).

N.6 What this file does NOT do

  • It does not present a proposed bill, amendment, proposed regulation, expired emergency regulation, or failed measure as current law. Pending items are labelled pending throughout (AB 2506, AB 1826, AB 1027, AB 1965, DCC-2025-01-R, DCC-2025-03-R, DCC-2026-03-R, DCC hemp rulemaking 2026–27 to 2027–28, DCC-2026-04-R permanent A/M split rule).
  • It does not give legal advice (Part 12 especially). Housing, insurance, custody and immigration passages are secondary-guide summaries for research, not advice for any person's situation.
  • It does not fill gaps with estimates: no official licence-stock total is invented, no Part 8 property is upgraded without a current official cross-check, no tribal list is built from secondary directories, and no 3 November 2026 election outcome is stated.
  • It does not reproduce personal phone numbers or emails of individuals; business official public contact details only.

End of complete file. Cut-off: 1 November 2026. No 3 November 2026 election result is included.
